# Local changes on top of the pure devs private clone

Base: `PlayersCommittee/gemp-swccg` @ `55c22cf49` (canonical devs repo, compiles clean).
Reference copy of the (broken) public release: `../gemp-swccg-public-PUBLIC-COPY-2026-06-22`.
Everything below is the ONLY divergence from pure devs code — each is reversible.

## 2026-07-19: V232 AI-ONLY MOVE WEAPON-HUNTER POLICY OWNER (both bots)
- Why: the V29.7 armed-character target scan remained duplicated in both `MoveEvaluator` mirrors. It classified attached weapons, estimated weapon and IHYN power, ranked every other opponent location, formatted the selected reason, and supplied the score used by the adapter's adjacency-gated ladder claim.
- Consolidation: shared `MoveWeaponHunterPolicy` now owns ordered attached-weapon title classification, effective-power facts, strict viability, target scoring, first-best selection, exact reasons, and selected-target ordinal. Both adapters retain the aggressive-solo outer gate, attachment/card-category reads, conditional IHYN hand scan and swallowed inner exception, ordered physical-location and opponent-card reads, icon-side mapping, physical target list, direct score/log application, engine adjacency query, R2 claim, remote-target log, broad outer catch, and original position between V29.9 readiness and SPREAD.
- Behavior boundary: this is structural only. The last attached `WEAPON` title remains the displayed weapon while any attached lightsaber supplies `+4`; other weapons supply `+2`; and IHYN supplies `+3` only for a title containing Vader. A target still requires a positive power-bearing opponent count and strict effective-power advantage. Scores remain base `+60`, plus `+40` at advantage `>=6` or `+20` at `>=3`, `+15` per opposing Force icon, and `+150` for Vader at a location containing a Luke title. Strict `>` keeps the first equal-scoring location. The historical global `foundLuke` quirk remains, so a later stronger non-Luke winner can still receive the CHALLENGE LUKE reason. A null-title winning location still suppresses output. Only an engine-adjacent selected target attempts the existing battle-seeking R2 claim; remote targets remain R1 fines.
- Known legacy boundaries preserved: the rule still sees attached physical weapons only, counts undercover opponent cards, scans every other location before the later adjacency check, ignores opponent weapon power in its target estimate, and treats IHYN as a flat heuristic. V29.9 readiness, generic attack/SPREAD, `MoveHuntTargetPolicy`, and battle-side weapon owners remain separate and untouched.
- Scope boundary: every production edit is under `gemp-swccg-server/.../ai/**`. No game engine, decision metadata, card, action, mediator, serializer, client, deck, or database source changed. All live card, board, icon, and adjacency reads remain adapter-owned.
- Verification: 272 focused MOVE policy/source/parity/resolver tests passed. The full reactor passed 1,449 tests with 0 failures, 0 errors, and 26 skipped. Package, normalized Rando/Chosen One mirrors, strict-order and cross-owner source guards, AI-source boundary, forbidden-symbol, compiled-artifact, diff, and independent source-review gates passed. The packaged server jar SHA-256 is `4cf28273d1f22c888e513fce2541d09b53c2424495a2687b3a05f2ee646d79c7`; the packaged `web.jar` SHA-256 is `faaf7aa546085d7ad346e4546d080f130546b09ddedca76dcc3cf115e1eaba28`. Runtime JVM loading and live-game proof remain separate deployment gates.
- Revert: revert the single V232 commit. V231 remains the prior MOVE unarmed-Vader boundary.

## 2026-07-15: V201 AI-ONLY UNSUPPORTED-SOLO DEFER TIER (both bots)
- Why: the L3 weak-solo rule was still an additive `-800`. Existing destination stacks could contribute about `+2050` before L3, leaving an unsupported solo at `+1250`; the forced-location pull route could remain at `+200`. Increasing the scalar would only move the next domination failure.
- Closed ordering: `EvaluatedAction` now OR-merges a non-additive `DEFER` marker. Both normal selection and the ordered deploy-bucket walk apply `ADMISSIBLE > DEFER > HARD_BLOCK` before comparing scores. A legal Pass or any admissible candidate beats every deferred action. Mandatory choices use the best admissible candidate, then the best deferred candidate, then the least-bad hard block, so the AI cannot hang.
- Plan evidence: a weak first body is admissible only when the detached AI deployment plan identifies another exact physical character still in hand at the same destination and the printed plan budget remains sufficient. Duplicate or unknown blueprint identity cannot inherit another instruction. Mere character presence and speculative later movement are not plans. Forced Reserve pulls claim no companion plan.
- Formation boundary: undercover deployment, the existing objective flip gate, current friendly support, ability at least 4, and existing dominance remain explicit allowances. An unsupported weak solo at an empty site is deferred; an unaffordable planned pair or weak solo into a contested site remains hard-blocked. The retained `-800` only ranks mandatory all-deferred fallbacks and can no longer be washed out by later bonuses.
- Honest scope: V201 closes the unsupported-solo scalar leak and shared candidate ordering only. It does not claim a complete engine-backed buddy transaction, exact movement sequence, survival-retreat obligation, or migration of global V169 rescue/contact/overpower weights. Those remain separate AI-only follow-ons.
- Engine boundary: every production edit is under `gemp-swccg-server/.../ai/**`. No game engine, card, logic, mediator, decision metadata, serializer, or client source changed.
- Verification: 65 focused formation, plan-identity, selection-order, and trace tests passed. The full affected reactor passed 928 tests with 0 failures and 0 errors (26 skipped). Rando and Chosen One changed streams normalize identically; the forbidden metadata scan and `git diff --check` are clean.
- Revert: revert the single V201 commit. No non-AI production source is part of the change.

## 2026-07-15: V200 AI-ONLY OBJECTIVE FRONT/BACK NORMALIZATION (both bots)
- Why: `ObjectiveAnalyzer` read only the physical card's current blueprint. Once an objective flipped, the analyzer could treat the back title and text as the front. Its back-side parser searched the current game text for a literal `[Back Side]` marker that appears in zero objective blueprints, so real flip-back text was never parsed.
- Stable sides: shared `ObjectiveSideBlueprints` derives front and back from the stock `getBlueprint()`, `getOtherSideBlueprint()`, and `isFlipped()` APIs. Both bots always analyze the front title/text and pass the real back game text to the existing flip-back parser. Single-sided cards retain front analysis and an absent current blueprint still fails closed.
- Boundary math: no score, threshold, candidate order, Pass/Done behavior, or parser magnitude changed. Correct back-side classification can now select existing rules that were previously fed front-side fallback locations. Audited per-candidate deltas range from `+200` objective protection to a theoretical `+1080` deploy branch swing; the existing V47 Lando-stay `-100000` veto can now arm when its stock survivability and location gates both pass. These are existing rule values, newly supplied with the intended back-side location facts.

- Revert: revert the V200 commit. No non-AI production source is part of the change.

## 2026-07-14: DEPLOY PHASE OWNER CUTOVER (Steve-approved, both bots)
- Why: one deployment could cross parent action, destination, simultaneous buddy, undercover, capacity, confirmation, and forced auto-selection paths while old evaluators and mutable planner state each held only part of its identity. This allowed stale cursors, physical-copy ambiguity, and contradictory parent/child safety decisions.
- `DeployPhaseOwner` now owns only complete origin-stamped DEPLOY wire shapes. `DeployFacts`, `DeployAssessment`, and `DeployTransaction` bind one opaque attempt id, diagnostic parent id and ordinal, exact current/permanent source identity, ordered destinations, exact buddy candidates, selected buddy, formation verdict, and unchanged `ForceObligationVector`. Prompt text and DEPLOY phase alone never claim ownership.
- Lifecycle changes occur only after engine acceptance: `SNAPSHOT -> PARENT_PENDING -> CHILD_PENDING -> COMMITTED -> COMPLETED`. Permanent physical identity survives engine current-id rotation. Expected source-to-deployed transitions complete once across attached, at-location, related-starship/vehicle, part-of-system, system-orbited, and converted-location relations. Rejection, cancellation, game/phase/turn drift, unexpected zone drift, and broken Force affordability terminate the exact attempt without affecting duplicate blueprint copies.
- Character parent construction performs one non-mutating legality preflight. A singleton destination therefore receives the parent formation assessment even when the engine skips the child prompt. The later wire distinguishes a true engine auto-selection from an optional singleton prompt. Zone deployments carry an exact typed zone destination.
- Simultaneous deployments carry exact buddy identity at the parent boundary. Hand buddy prompts preserve CARD_SELECTION current-card wires and ARBITRARY `tempN` wires separately from current/permanent physical ids. Early-created confirmation decisions refresh accepted parent identity immediately before engine delivery.
- Direct `decide()` and mediator execution now share one acceptance lifecycle without double advancement. Direct calls accept the selected parent or PULL child immediately; mediator calls defer the same transition until the engine acceptance callback.
- PULL retains search, target, and outcome ownership. Its destination origin is no longer overwritten by DEPLOY metadata. The accepted PULL deploy child starts only DEPLOY's Force and sequencing cursor, defers formation until the first exact destination child, then binds that accepted immutable assessment once.
- `ObjectiveDeployAdapter` is now the exclusive live emitter for My Lord V83/V88/V108/V110, objective-site `+200`, and V193 parent `+400`/child `+2000`. Exact predecessors remain present but disabled. V99, V86/V121, formation policy, targeted rescue, legal overpower, and V79b's documented Rando-only waiver remain separate.
- Both old prompt-text V170 interceptors are disabled. Exact `PlayCharacterAction` provenance, ordered Yes/No labels, and known opponent drain feed the typed owner; unknown or malformed facts remain legacy-unowned.
- Optional structural or score-pseudo-veto choices preserve legal Pass/cancel; mandatory all-veto choices preserve the least-bad exact compatibility wire. Equal scores retain original candidate order. Cancellation blocks only the exact opaque attempt; engine rejection clears without poisoning replay.
- Formation presence is tri-state and physical, not ability-derived: an ability-zero friendly body is PRESENT, known empty is ABSENT, and missing or unresolved board facts remain UNKNOWN. The common forced-destination assessment preserves the unflipped named first-pull exemption, exact post-flip unsupported-repeat and weak-solo penalties, UNKNOWN identity, and true hard blocks for both bots.
- One immutable `ForceObligationVector` is created for the decision and reused by DEPLOY parent, child, Pass, and movement-preservation policy. Assessment copies deep-copy deployment instructions, and completed deployments validate the actual destination before terminating the exact transaction.
- Regression corrections: direct fixtures can have no bound `SwccgGame`; `planForDecision` returns an accepted assessment copy or unknown `null` instead of inventing board facts. Temporary PULL child wires resolve by ordinal to exact physical cards, and unexpected destination drift terminates once.
- Boundary: 185 focused DEPLOY/objective/PULL/snapshot/safety/intent/parity tests plus 100 mediator, mirrored-bot, trace, PULL-integration, and score-parity regressions passed with zero failures or errors. The full V170 direct/mediator/order/unknown/malformed matrix, named strategic intents, rotated-id lifecycle, location relations, exact blocked replay, and deferred PULL formation are pinned for both bots. Affected server reactor package passed, all six repaired mirrored file-pair streams normalized identically on top of the original nine-pair gate, and `git diff --check` is clean. No game simulation, push, or deployment occurred during the gate.
- Retained source: old objective and V170 predecessor bodies remain disabled in place for the later physical-removal phase. Deploy weights and solo-plan penalties are intentionally unchanged and remain a separate approved phase.
- Revert: revert the single DEPLOY phase commit.

## 2026-07-14: OBJECTIVE FACTS AND ADAPTER PHASE (Steve-approved, both bots)
- Why: objective identity, profile lookup, and phase policy were read from mutable analyzer state at several evaluator sites. Physical flip orientation and same-opponent rematches could therefore reuse incorrect facts, while objective PULL scoring still depended on separate parent and child branches.
- Every mediated decision now builds one immutable `ObjectiveFacts` view before trace selection. `DecisionSnapshot` version 4 carries that exact instance; trace, `CombinedEvaluator`, and all objective adapters consume it without rebuilding or refreshing analyzer state. UNKNOWN facts remain UNKNOWN and the snapshot contains no score, rank, veto, `ScoreNote`, or playbook weights.
- Physical identity uses `PhysicalCard.getBlueprint()` plus `getOtherSideBlueprint()` and exact current/opposite blueprint ids. Canonical front/back identity remains stable across a physical flip. Objective mutable state resets only when the `SwccgGame` object reference changes, so same-game snapshot/revert retains state and a same-opponent rematch does not.
- Runtime profile resolution is blueprint-first, title compatibility second, then the compiled My Lord/Endor fallback when no usable JSON profile exists. Rando and ChosenOne use mirrored analyzers and the same immutable projection.
- Five narrow common adapters now own objective translation. DEPLOY, MOVE, BATTLE, and SETUP are observation-only shadow paths. DEPLOY shadows the closed My Lord V83/V88/V108/V110, objective-site `+200`, and V193 parent `+400`/child `+2000` set while leaving V99, V86, V121, formation, affordability, legality, and sequencing with their existing owners. SETUP reads only typed starting-location and starting-interrupt origins.
- `ObjectivePullAdapter` is authoritative for the objective-source parent `+1500`, child-location `+500`, and canonical failed-verify intent. ARBITRARY child wire ids such as `temp0` resolve by ordinal through the transaction's ordered physical-card identities, never through `GameState.findCardById`. When immutable objective identity is unavailable or its ids disagree but the physical source is typed as an objective, the adapter preserves the exact legacy parent score and the predecessor child route preserves its exact legacy rank; blocked assessments never fall back. The replaced parent and child emitters remain physically present but disabled for the later caller-proof phase. The engine PULL transaction remains the only failed-search truth; no title-keyed failed-pull map was recreated.
- Hidden Path uses two typed non-Mapuzo sites occupied by Jedi. Underground Corridor emits Jedi Survivor movement intent with cost default `1`. Hunt intent uses typed Inquisitor and stacked Hatred membership. These adapters emit intent only and do not decide phase legality.
- Revert: revert the single objective facts/adapters phase commit. Retained legacy DEPLOY/MOVE/BATTLE/SETUP scoring remains live because those adapters are shadow; the disabled objective PULL predecessors are adjacent to their authoritative adapter call sites.
- Phase boundary: 173 focused tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped; affected reactor package success; `git diff --check` clean; six mirrored evaluator/analyzer files normalized identical. Independent audits caught the temporary-wire child seam and the identity-mismatch fallback gap before deployment. The final corpus covers the real `ArbitraryCardsSelectionDecision` metadata seam, typed ordinal-to-physical-card mapping, exact legacy fallback values, known-id mismatch, and blocked-assessment non-fallback.
- Deployment disposition: no game simulation and no push. Local reload follows the phase commit only after the no-live-game gate.

## 2026-07-13 — FINALIZER-RUNTIME + ACCEPTED-RESPONSE LIFECYCLE PREREQUISITE (Steve-APPROVED engine phase, Codex packet bc430fee) — record-after-acceptance, behavior-NEUTRAL, capture off
- FIRST behavioral-migration ENGINE phase (Steve approved all planned phases 2026-07-13; "pull the trigger on all the phases"). This phase moves the outer Rando/ChosenOne tracker + strategic-event commit to AFTER engine acceptance. It is the runtime seam every later phase owner submits a FinalizedResponse through; it does NOT cut over ACTIVATE/CONTROL or make the pure finalizer authoritative.
- NEW (3 main): AiDecisionResult (typed WIRE_RESPONSE/TYPED_REJECTION envelope, accepted-mutation mode NONE/OUTER_COMMON, optional tracker-mutation request), DecisionRejectionKind (ENGINE_REJECTED/TYPED_REJECTION/ATTEMPT_FAILED), FinalizedResponseAdapter (pure FinalizedResponse->AiDecisionResult; never applies the mutation). + 6 new focused tests.
- SwccgAiController: keep 3-arg decide() for direct callers; add 3-arg decideForEngine (default wraps decide as legacy NONE) + history-aware 4-arg decideForEngine(...,RejectionHistory) (4-arg default delegates to 3-arg so no override is bypassed) + onDecisionAccepted/onDecisionRejected(kind,detail)/onDecisionAttemptFailed callbacks. RejectionHistory gains immutable append() + ENGINE_DECISION_INVALID reason (ResponseFinalizer never emits it).
- SwccgGameMediator (AI PATH ONLY; human playerAnswered path byte-for-byte unchanged): calls only decideForEngine (:1373); mediator owns RejectionHistory.empty() as a loop-local (counts 0 then 1, exact rejected wire + ENGINE_DECISION_INVALID on retry), never in a map/field/numeric-id/ThreadLocal; engine acceptance LATCHES after decisionMade returns, THEN onDecisionAccepted (:1443) BEFORE carryOutPendingActionsUntilDecisionNeeded (:1469) + startClocksForUsersPendingDecision (:1470); accepted-callback fault is logged + the accepted path continues (never rejection/second-submit/retry); ATTEMPT_FAILED replaces the forbidden TraceSession.abandon() for compute/wrapper (no-result) and decisionMade-runtime-fault (result-existed) throws.
- RandoCalAi + TheChosenOneAi: decide()/decideForEngine split — decide() still applies the outer common mutation inline (direct callers unchanged); decideForEngine returns OUTER_COMMON without applying it; the accepted callback applies recordDecision + trackStrategicEvents (before-snapshot moved WITH the record) + records the accepted wire + closes. Trace close is CALL-PATH-aware not mutation-mode-aware: direct decide() closes inline; every mediator-facing result INCLUDING mode NONE defers close to the disposition callback (Curator can override a NONE/V45 wire). Mirror parity EXACT (149 identical normalized lines each).
- TraceFinalization/TraceCollector/TraceSession: disposition-aware completeness — proposed-wire + recorded flag, one closed Disposition (ENGINE_ACCEPTED/ENGINE_REJECTED/TYPED_REJECTION/ATTEMPT_FAILED), MutationMode, mutation-completed flag; finalResponse populated ONLY on ENGINE_ACCEPTED (or direct call); rejected/failed close COMPLETE without a fabricated accepted response; genuine capture/mutation fault stays INCOMPLETE.
- Curator: history-aware decideForEngine forwards the SAME immutable history + accept/reject/attempt-failed callbacks to the wrapped Rando (exactly once each); package-private ctor injecting the wrapped RandoCalAi + a PURE package-private applyOverride helper (production consult uses it); no network in tests; USE_MODEL/callOllama untouched.
- HeuristicAiBase: NOT touched — its shared-base loop memory (tracker/recent-response/reassignment/failed-search) stays PRE-accept as the declared residual for chaos + heuristic-fallback routes.
- BEHAVIOR-NEUTRAL: no wire response, evaluator score, route, candidate order, RNG draw, retry count, or game policy change. Human path unchanged. Capture default stays NoOpTraceSink.INSTANCE (both bots :146). ResponseFinalizer STILL has NO production phase-owner caller (the V44/V67j pilot, step 1b, is the first).
- Codex's independent source gate caught FOUR load-bearing contract contradictions in the first released packet (m00579) before code, then caught two lifecycle leaks in the first runtime commit (m00603): a requeue-dispatch throw could skip ENGINE_REJECTED, and an accepted outer-mutation failure could lose the accepted wire. The amendment closes the rejection in a requeue-then-callback finally block and records the accepted wire before marking mutation capture incomplete. Three exact regressions cover both bots and the mediator. Focused phase verify: 216 tests / 0 fail / 0 error / 0 skip, BUILD SUCCESS; guard-order + mediator-only-decideForEngine + no-mediator-pre-accept-recordDecision + interceptor-NONE + parity + NoOpTraceSink static proofs all pass; git diff --check clean.
- TRANSPARENT WATCH-POINT (flagged for Codex aggregate gate, not a hard stop): moving recordDecision post-accept means a checked-rejected attempt no longer writes the OUTER tracker. DecisionTracker.checkSequenceLoop short-circuits at sequence<4, so an omitted single record cannot flip loop detection / getBlockedResponses on any normal/tested path; the only divergence is an already-looping game, where NOT recording an engine-invalid response is this phase's intended/correct behavior. Wire provably identical on every tested path.

## 2026-07-11 — FORMATION SAFETY: Steve's four laws as un-outvotable VETOES on every route (both bots)
- THE structural fix for the ~20x-recurring solo/low-ability class. Codex root-cause audit (Handoffs/CODEX_SOLO_ABILITY_ROOT_CAUSE_AUDIT_2026-07-11.md) + council review both confirmed: the basics were additive penalties (-150..-500) that the R2 +6000 move band and +600 bonus stacks outvote (H2), living on only some of the five scoring routes (H1).
- NEW `common/strategy/FormationSafety.java` — SHARED by both bots (like CharacterDeploySiteEvaluator: one copy, cannot mirror-drift). The four laws: L1 never abandon a lone weak ally (enemy present, not-doomed origin); L2 never voluntarily battle without a normal battle-destiny draw (ENGINE truth: total ability >= 4, BattleDestiny.java:31-91 — ability parity does NOT draw); L3 never land a weak (ability<4) solo when an affordable buddy is in hand; L4 never move/deploy a weak solo into an enemy-held site beyond the doomed gap. EXEMPTIONS (explicit): V172 solo dominance (>=2x weapon-adjusted, characters only), V193-class flip-gate steer, undercover spies, destiny-eligible solos at uncontested sites, survival retreat from a doomed origin (V33 gap>=6). All weapon math TYPED (Icon.PERMANENT_WEAPON — Codex m00172: game-text substring false-positived "mentions" cards).
- MOD `EvaluatedAction` (both bots) — `hardVeto(reason)`/`isHardVetoed()`, OR-merged in mergeFrom (no bonus stack can wash a veto out).
- MOD `CombinedEvaluator` (both bots) — vetoed actions are UN-SELECTABLE at both selection points (DPS bucket walk + final max). All-vetoed + pass legal → PASS; all-vetoed + must choose → least-bad (never hang; DecisionSafety doctrine).
- WIRED (facts-complete routes only — council rule: never veto blind): CS deploy-site block (L3/L4; Greedo incident), CS move-destination (L1/L4 via actual mover + origin; Chiraneau/Tarkin/Hondo incidents), BattleEvaluator specific branch + locationless fallback (L2; First Light incident). MoveEvaluator/ATE routes converge on the same CS destination sub-decision, which now vetoes.
- ALSO: V171/V172 gated to CardCategory.CHARACTER (Codex H3: V171 fired on the STARSHIP First Light, borrowing hand-character wave power for +600).
- Boundary (Codex's 8 verification scenarios): Greedo→solo site vetoed while buddies affordable; First Light battle vetoed (ability 3 < 4); Chiraneau leaving Ozzel vetoed (L1); Tarkin into Rey vetoed (L4 gap); Hondo into Anakin+Yoda vetoed (L4); Tyranus 8 vs lone Leia 3 ALLOWED (dominance); V193 flip-gate steer ALLOWED; spy deploys ALLOWED.
- Verified: compiles clean both bots (MVN_EXIT=0); DEPLOYED + byte-verified. work-verifier + live-game proof next.
- Revert: `git revert`; the veto layer is additive (remove the 5 wire points + class); all old additive rules still present.

## 2026-07-10 — Rey-game fixes wave 2 (replay rbujmoc90br3uu4c, both bots): battle weapon/hit economics + retreat unblocking + pull-veto integrity
- ALL adjust-in-place. Completes the Rey-game fix set (wave 1 below). Common thread: every power comparison was blind to opponent WEAPONS and the hit mechanic while Rando's own projections counted his sabers.
- **V29.7/V76 opponent weapons** (`BattleEvaluator` specific branch): the V29.7 loop now also sums the OPPONENT's weapon bonus (lightsaber +5/other +3, attached + permanent) and V76's `predictBattle` receives `theirPower + oppWeaponBonus` (was raw). The predictor no longer sees our sabers but not theirs.
- **V76 in the V22.4 fallback** (`BattleEvaluator` fallback loop — the route "Initiate battle for free" actually takes; V76 previously NEVER ran here): fallback now weapon-adjusts `effectiveDiff` by the opponent's weapon bonus AND prices HIT ECONOMICS — each armed opponent character ≈ one of our characters forfeited at 0 (avg forfeit of our characters present); expected card loss ≥ 10 → −500 pyrrhic (V76's bar) and the location can never be "favorable". Boundary (T5 incident): 19v13 "+40 favorable" now either pyrrhic-vetoed (2-3 armed opps × avg forfeit ~4) or effectiveDiff-negative after +8-10 opp weapons → "no favorable battles". Clean fights vs unarmed opponents unchanged (hitLoss=0, oppWeap=0). NOTE: implemented at the call sites, NOT inside BattlePredictor's Monte Carlo (smaller blast radius than the original plan).
- **V47 + V37.1 weapon-adjusted threat** (`MoveEvaluator`): the rankMoveFromLocation power loop and V47 LANDO STAY survivability now weapon-adjust opponent power (new `oppWeaponBonusAt` helper / inline sum). T5 incident: Lando 6v8-raw read "survivable −2 → RISKY-hold −500"; now 6v11 armed → −5..−6 → past `BATTLE_DANGER_THRESHOLD` → RETREAT tier. Thresholds themselves unchanged.
- **V67ae retreat exemption** (`ActionTextEvaluator`): mirrors V33 BUDDY BREAK's "hopelessly outgunned (gap ≥ 6) → allow retreat" — location-sourced move actions never reach MoveEvaluator's tiers, so the −300 non-drain tax now stands down when ANY friendly-occupied location is doomed (`V67ae RETREAT EXEMPT` log). Normal drain-abandonment still penalized.
- **V177 word-rescue type-word skip** (`ActionTextEvaluator`): the ≥6-char word rescue no longer accepts GENERIC TYPE WORDS (via `DeckOracle.mapTypeWordToCategory`, visibility widened) — "lightsaber" alone no longer revives "leia's lightsaber" pulls (incident 5's second hole).
- **V185 ATE mirror** (`ActionTextEvaluator` V67h WILL_SUCCEED branch): twin drift fixed — the Deploy-side V185 no-holder weapon veto (−2000) was additively diluted by ATE's V192 (+1575, net −375). The same `reserveTargetsAreAllUnattachableWeapons` gate now hard-blocks (−9999) on the ATE route too.

## 2026-07-10 — Rey-game fixes wave 1 (replay rbujmoc90br3uu4c, both bots): V177/V166 gate, V22.3 tiers, V148 deploy-cancel, V67s/V185/V82.1/V82.2 pull guards
- ALL adjust-in-place (no new V-tags). Root causes from two independent replay/log analyses + Codex corpus checks (m00118).
- **V177 gate in V166 CONTEST DRAIN** (`CardSelectionEvaluator` ~884): survivability math was optimistic-self vs discounted-enemy — counted a saber-in-hand wave (+5) with ZERO affordable buddies and used raw enemy power blind to weapons. Now: wave counts only with ≥1 affordable buddy (`wave[1]>=1`, V172's standard) AND enemy power is weapon-adjusted (new `v177OppWeaponBonus`: lightsaber +5/other +3, attached or permanent — V29.7 heuristic). Boundary: T2 Yoda case 3 vs 18 → GATED (was 8 vs 8 → +350 lure → Yoda died, 8 damage).
- **V22.3 contested tiers** (~2735): boundaries now INCLUSIVE (>=5/-150, >=10/-250, >=15/-350) — a gap of exactly 10 drew only -150 vs the +830 contest stack.
- **V148 deploy-cancel bar** (`CombinedEvaluator` ~283): for "where to deploy" prompts the cancel bar is now < 0 (was −100) — Luke TLJ was committed to a −10 site when ALL sites scored negative and Done was available. Deploying to a negative site is strictly worse than holding the card. All other decisions keep −100.
- **V67s possessive guard** (`DeckOracle.hasTargetInZone` ~950): the last-word type fallback made "leia's lightsaber" match Anakin's Lightsaber → validated a pull whose real target was in the Force Pile. Skipped for genuine "X's <type>" targets (new `isPossessiveTypeTarget`; Codex m00118: icon remnants like "jabba's palace lando" keep the rescue).
- **V185 gate integrity** (`DeckOracle.reserveTargetsAreAllUnattachableWeapons` ~485): same possessive guard + a FUZZY last-word hit on a NON-weapon (Ahsoka Tano With Lightsabers, a character) may no longer stand the no-holder gate down — only exact/stripped title matches may. Fixes 3 dead saber pulls (incidents 2-4).
- **V82.1 parser** (`DeckOracle.parseSourceCardPullTargets`): 4th pattern `search your Reserve Deck … take/choose X into hand` — Clash Of Sabers' search text parsed to ZERO targets so V177 never checked the deck; Rando burned 1 Force + the interrupt searching for Uncontrollable Fury which was never in the deck. Codex corpus: 37/41 texts match; 4 legit search modes stay unmatched → fail open (V177 skips).
- **V82.2 predicate fallback** (`DeckOracle.validatePullFromSourceCard`): WILL_SUCCEED now requires ALL words recognized ("jedi village" no longer validates off 'Rey, All Of The Jedi'); partially-recognized targets return UNKNOWN, not WILL_FAIL (Codex m00118: protects "maintenance droid"/"non-unique Rebel" generic pulls). NEW **V82.2b persona rescue**: targets like "reflections ii chewie" resolve via the engine Persona enum against zone blueprints — kills the 40+ false-blocks of the LIVE Chewbacca, Protector pull (possessive tokens excluded).

## 2026-07-10 — SAFETY CLAMP + V22.7 routing fix: "Any Methods Necessary" setup hang (both bots)
- Root cause (replay `2jg1sj0l3qrlgy6a`, 2026-07-10 17:36): Any Methods Necessary (109_7) fires an ITERATIVE take-combination decision (`TakeCardCombinationIntoHandFromPileEffect`, min=max=1/round, returnAnyChange). The V22.7 `matching+starship` routing catch-all in CardSelectionEvaluator (~L417) misrouted it to `evaluatePilotSelection`, which ignores the `selectable[]` array (and can't parse temp ids), answering a NON-selectable card. Engine throws `DecisionResultInvalidException`; `SwccgGameMediator.maybeLetAiPlay` SWALLOWS it for AI players with no log and no retry → decision pends forever = silent CPU-idle hang. Chain verified vs engine source (ArbitraryCardsSelectionDecision:241-266, SwccgGameMediator:1325-1329).
- FIX 1 (class-level, both bots) `.../{rando,chosenone}/DecisionSafety.java` — SAFETY LAYER 2b in `ensureValidResponse` (the boundary every decide() passes, same philosophy as the 2026-05-31 MULTI-SELECT fix): for ARBITRARY_CARDS/CARD_SELECTION, drop answer tokens that are unknown or `selectable=false`; if survivors < required, rebuild as all `preselected` ids + the FIRST selectable non-preselected id (ONE new per round when returnAnyChange — mirrors the human click flow; filling several can build an impossible combination), cap at max; log `SAFETY CLAMP`. Any future evaluator that answers a non-selectable card now degrades to a logged safe pick instead of hanging the game.
- FIX 2 (adjust V22.7 IN PLACE, both bots) `.../evaluators/CardSelectionEvaluator.java` (~L417) — the matching/starship catch-all now excludes `into hand`/`prison` texts, so take-into-hand combinations no longer route to pilot logic (AMSD pilot texts unaffected: they're deploy-aboard, not into-hand).
- NOT done (needs Steve's explicit ask — engine file): `SwccgGameMediator.maybeLetAiPlay` still swallows `DecisionResultInvalidException` for AI players with no logging/retry. A LOG.error + one emergency-retry there would make this whole hang CLASS impossible. Flagged for Steve.
- Verified: compiles clean both bots (MVN_EXIT=0). Repro: dark Endor/AMN deck reaches PLAY_STARTING_CARDS → expect `SAFETY CLAMP`/normal pick + prison/bounty-hunter into hand over 2-4 rounds instead of a hang.
- Revert: `git revert`; both edits are self-contained (clamp block + one routing condition).

For each parsed pull-target still in Reserve: if it is a non-weapon → not all-weapons (don't block); if a weapon → read its OWN `getMatchingCharacterFilter()` and test whether any of Rando's in-play characters satisfies it via `Filters.filterActive(game, null, and(owner, character, filter))`. A weapon whose filter is `Filters.none` (deploys via game text) is deferred, not blocked. Robust title matching mirrors `hasTargetInZone` inline. (First pass `hasCharacterInPlay()` + `reserveTargetsAreAllWeapons()` — the cruder "any character" check — left commented above per the comment-out-superseded-logic rule.)
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/evaluators/DeployEvaluator.java` — in the V67h `WILL_SUCCEED` branch (~line 798, the live path that scored A Good Friend), after `MEMORY OK`: `v185Game = context.getGame(); if (v185Game != null && reserveTargetsAreAllUnattachableWeapons(v185Game, playerId, parseSourceCardPullTargets(srcGameText)))` → block the pull (`-2000`) and `continue`.
- Why: V67h only verifies a pull target is IN the Reserve Deck, not that it can DEPLOY. `A Good Friend` (225_37) deploys one of {location, epic event, Leia's Lightsaber}; once the location + epic event are down, only the lightsaber remains, and a weapon attaches only to the SPECIFIC characters its own filter accepts. With no such character on the table the deploy has no legal target, so the pull FAILS — wasting the action and revealing/reshuffling the Reserve. Reported by Steve (this lost Rando the game): "no one was on table that could hold a lightsaber, so the pull failed. If Rando had waited for characters to deploy first, he would have been successful."
- REFINED 2026-06-23 (same session, pre-deploy, in place — no new V-tag per the update-old-rule rule): the first pass gated on "Rando has zero characters of ANY kind." Steve flagged this as too crude — a weapon attaches only to specific characters (Leia's Lightsaber → Leia/Ben Solo/Rey ability > 4; Anakin's Lightsaber → Skywalker ability > 3), so Rando can have bodies out yet none able to hold THIS weapon and the pull still dead-fails. Now reads each weapon's own `getMatchingCharacterFilter()` — universal, no hardcoded card/character names.
- Boundary: fires ONLY when EVERY remaining Reserve target is a weapon with NO in-play character its own filter accepts. A non-weapon target still pullable (location/epic event), a weapon with a legal holder already down, or a game-text (`Filters.none`) weapon we can't predict, all leave the pull untouched. `-2000` matches V177's dead-pull penalty (drops below the V60 `+100` baseline; not the absolute `-9999` of a no-target search, leaving a safety valve if the heuristic over-fires). Blocks logged loudly (`V185 WEAPON-NO-HOLDER blocked: ...`).
- Verified: compiles clean (`mvn -pl gemp-swccg-server -am compile`, BUILD SUCCESS) + a 6-agent traced/adversarial review (verdict GO). Confirmed against source: `getMatchingCharacterFilter()` is on `SwccgCardBlueprint:1670` (base impl throws → category-gated to weapons), defaults to `Filters.none`; the matching filter is a pure predicate over the CANDIDATE character (the weapon's own card is never an input), so it evaluates correctly while the weapon is still in Reserve (mirrors `CardTraits.isMatchingCharacter:279`). Leia's Lightsaber → Leia/Ben Solo/Rey ability>4 (`Card225_050.java:41`); Anakin's Lightsaber → Skywalker ability>3 (`Card3_071.java:44`); F-11D Blaster Rifle uses game-text targets with `Filters.none` matching filter → deferred. STILL pending: the live in-game run — the running jar is old code until rebuilt.
- Not yet mirrored: a parallel `chosenone/` copy of `DeckOracle` + `DeployEvaluator` exists (convention: gates are mirrored). `RandoCalAi` is the live AI, so the rando copy is what fixes Steve's game; the chosenone mirror is a pending follow-up.
- Revert: remove `reserveTargetsAreAllUnattachableWeapons` + `hasInPlayCharacterAccepting` from `DeckOracle` and the V185 block in `DeployEvaluator` (both tagged `V185`); the commented-out first-pass helpers above show the prior state.
- NOTE: this install has no `AI_VERSION_HISTORY.md` (the V21–V184 V-tag history lives in the backups / `k2-resources`); the usual "update BOTH changelogs" rule is satisfied here by this single live changelog.

## 2026-06-23 — Rando V186: I Want That Map starting setup (Starkiller Base system + The First Order Was Just The Beginning)
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/evaluators/CardSelectionEvaluator.java` `evaluateDeployLocation` (~line 813) — THE location fix. The objective's turn-0 "Choose [Episode VII] location to deploy" is an `ArbitraryCardsSelectionDecision` whose cardIds are temp IDs (`temp0`...). The candidate loop resolves cards via `findCardById(Integer.parseInt(cardId))`, which THROWS for temp IDs before any scoring runs, so every Episode VII candidate kept only the flat +50 base and the pick was effectively RANDOM (the real root cause of Steve's wrong-location report). Added a temp-safe block: resolve each candidate's blueprint from the index-parallel `context.getBlueprints()` list and add +400 to the Starkiller Base SYSTEM (blueprint `208_51`). Gated on `cardId.startsWith("temp")` plus the active objective title containing "i want that map". The action is created (+50) BEFORE the throwing `parseInt`, and `actions.add(action)` is OUTSIDE the try/catch, so the +400 survives. System scores 450 vs 50 for its sites (`208_52..55`) and every other candidate, so it wins decisively. Names the SYSTEM only (its sites are different blueprint IDs) because the system's once-per-turn [download] fetches the SB battleground sites that feed the flip.
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/evaluators/CardSelectionEvaluator.java` `evaluateUnknown` turn-0 starting-effect block (~line 7960) — THE effect fix. +1000 to `the first order was just the beginning` when the active objective is I Want That Map. The starting interrupt You Know What I've Come For (208_46) deploys "one Effect immune to Alter"; this effect downloads Jakku/Kijimi battlegrounds to feed the 2-battleground flip. +1000 matches the V80 magnitude; the effect scores ~1100 vs ~100 for the next-best immune-to-Alter Effect, so it wins.
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/strategy/ObjectiveAnalyzer.java` `parseFlipCondition` (after the V160 block) — supporting block, gated on objective title "i want that map": `addLocationFragment("starkiller base")` (marks Starkiller locations objective-relevant for the +150 bonus on LATER real-id deploy/move/protection paths; it is INERT for the turn-0 temp-id pick itself, which the evaluator block above handles) plus adds "the first order was just the beginning" to `requiredCardsOnTable` and `pullableCards` (loss/forfeit protection).
- Why: for the dark "I Want That Map / And Now You'll Give It To Me" objective (208_57) Rando set up wrong. Card text: deploy Tuanul Village + "any other [Episode VII] location" + I Will Finish; flip when First Order controls two battlegrounds with no Resistance Agent at a battleground site. Correct picks: Starkiller Base SYSTEM (208_51) as the other location (its [download] builds the battlegrounds) and The First Order Was Just The Beginning (214_12) as the starting Effect. Neither was named anywhere; the live `ObjectiveAnalyzer` is text-driven and the flip text names no card, and the Starkiller SYSTEM has NO battleground icon so a generic battleground heuristic misses it. (NOTE: the dead `ObjectiveHandler.OBJECTIVE_REQUIREMENTS` map had a 208_57 entry but is never consulted anywhere in `src/`, verified codebase-wide; editing it is a no-op.)
- Scope/boundary: all three blocks gated to the objective title containing "i want that map", so no other objective is touched. The +400 is additionally gated to temp IDs, so later real-id deploys do NOT get steered onto the system over the battleground sites. The ObjectiveAnalyzer fragment applies +150 to both the system and its sites on later real-id paths (on-strategy: build the battlegrounds).
- Verified: compiles clean (`mvn -pl gemp-swccg-server -am compile`, BUILD SUCCESS in container). Two adversarial workflows. The first 4-lens pass CAUGHT that the original analyzer-fragment-only approach was inert (the temp-id `parseInt` throw) and that a +150 would have lost the system-vs-site tie by +30. The corrected evaluator approach was re-verified by a 3-lens pass (does-it-fire end-to-end, magnitude, regression), all PASS, with confirmed score math (system 450 vs 50, effect 1100 vs 100), confirmed context population (`setBlueprints` at RandoCalAi ~943, index-parallel; `setTestingTexts` is never called so the blueprint-id match carries the fix), and confirmed no cross-evaluator merge onto `ARBITRARY_CARDS` candidates. STILL pending: the live in-game run. The running jar is old code until rebuilt.
- Revert: remove the three `V186`-tagged blocks (the `evaluateDeployLocation` temp-safe Starkiller block, the `evaluateUnknown` First Order +1000 branch, and the `ObjectiveAnalyzer.parseFlipCondition` named block).

## 2026-06-25 — Cancel-loop deploy fix: turn-scope the decision-block maps (Rando stopped deploying after turn 2)
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/DecisionTracker.java` — two writes to the PERMANENT `blockedResponses` map redirected to the TURN-SCOPED `turnBlockedActions`: (1) `blockLastActionOnCancel` (~line 443) — comment out the `blockedResponses` write, KEEP the existing `turnBlockedActions` write at ~446; (2) the sequence-repeat detector (~line 302) — comment out the `blockedResponses` write, ADD a `turnBlockedActions` write with the same key/response.
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/chosenone/DecisionTracker.java` — identical mirror (both write sites). chosenone's `:302` had NO turn-scoped sibling, so it was the most exposed copy.
- Why: Steve played a DARK Battle-Droid swarm deck; Rando deployed turns 1-2 then STOPPED, drew into a 13-20 card hand with 27 force, and never built ground presence. Root cause: the cancel-loop guard. A lone Battle Droid (ability 0) fails the V136 §A ability gate (-1500 at every site), so the "Choose where to deploy" pick scores all-negative, Rando clicks Done, and after 3 strikes `blockLastActionOnCancel` wrote the blocked deploy slot into the PERMANENT `blockedResponses` map. That map clears only at game start (mid-game `onPhaseChange` resets the WRONG DecisionTracker instance — RandoCalAi overrides `decide()` and reads its own tracker, which `onPhaseChange` never touches). The block keys on a POSITIONAL action ID the engine REUSES for a different card each decision, so a slot blocked by an un-placeable droid on turn 4 vetoed the legitimate Lord Sidious (A:7) and Dofine+Tey How (A:5) group deploys the planner actually wanted on turn 5. By turn 5 the accumulated set `{3,4,5,7,8,9,10,11}` `-9999`'d every Deploy action; even `V37.4 HAND BLOAT: DEPLOY SOMETHING!` was hard-vetoed.
- Fix mechanism: `getBlockedResponses()` returns the UNION of both maps, so writing to `turnBlockedActions`-only preserves within-turn loop protection (the 3-strike detector still re-trips fresh each turn); `turnBlockedActions.clear()` runs on every turn change (`updateState`, verified to run on RandoCalAi's OWN tracker before the deploy gate reads), so the block no longer survives into later turns. Both permanent writes (cancel-loop 443 + sequence-repeat 302) closed, so the whole permanent-write class is fixed, not half.
- Boundary/scope: every consumer of the block map (`DeployEvaluator`, `MoveEvaluator`, `ActionTextEvaluator`, `DrawEvaluator`, `HeuristicAiBase`) uses it for WITHIN-turn loop-breaking; none relies on cross-turn persistence (`BattleEvaluator`/`CardSelectionEvaluator` don't read it). The cross-turn severity ladder (`getLoopSeverity`/`shouldForceDifferentChoice`/`shouldConsiderConcede`) keys off `sequenceRepeatCount`, NOT this map, so cross-turn loop protection is untouched.
- Verified: adversarial read-only review (GO-WITH-CHANGES verdict — `Handoffs/K2_HANDOFF_2026-06-25_fix-review-verdict.md`) + local council (engineer + voice_of_reason, both confirmed Fix sound + the 302 catch). Empirically 302 never poisoned a deploy in the logged game (14 fires, all non-deploy phases; the turn-5 lockout was 100% the 443 path, 35 `CANCEL LOOP BROKEN` events) — fixed anyway as a same-class landmine. STILL PENDING: live in-game droid-deck run (grep `nohup.out`: turn-5+ deploy is NOT a wall of `-9999`, a droid lands). The running jar is old code until rebuilt.
- Does NOT close: within a SINGLE turn the positional-ID collision still survives (a deploy phase can accumulate slot-IDs that wall off a good card the same turn). Real cure = key the block on the cancelled card's blueprint/title instead of the reused positional slot — follow-up V-tag, not shipped here.
- Pairs with (NOW APPLIED 2026-06-25, see the §A entry below): the §A droid/fodder fix (`CharacterDeploySiteEvaluator.java` ~360-365, gate the -1500 on `oppPower > 0`), which stops droids entering the loop at the source.
- Revert: un-comment the four `blockedResponses.computeIfAbsent(...)` lines (rando + chosenone, at ~302 and ~443) and remove the added `turnBlockedActions` write at ~302 in both files.

## 2026-06-25 — Fix #2: V136 §A ability penalty gated to contested sites only (fodder seeds uncontested + objective sites)
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/common/strategy/CharacterDeploySiteEvaluator.java` (~360-365) — the §A `-1500` "almost never deploy a sub-ability-4 group" gate now fires only when `oppPower > 0` (contested). At an UNCONTESTED site (`oppPower == 0`) a low-ability fodder body (Battle Droids ability 0, weak Neimoidians) falls through to V156 (turn≤2 solo guard) then the `+500` reward, so it deploys to establish presence/drain and to SEED objective flip-sites. Shared `common/` file → fixes both bots.
- Why: a droid swarm has zero-ability bodies, so §A `-1500`'d EVERY ground deploy (contested or not) — Rando couldn't build presence, and could never seed the Invasion objective's Theed Palace Throne Room (V22 boosted the site `+150` but §A `-1500` dominated → net `-1200`, room stayed empty, objective never flipped). Steve: droids are fodder; low ability is fine at an empty site you just want to drain.
- Boundary math (additive-domination discipline): uncontested ability<4 goes `-1500` → `+500` (or V156 `-300` on turn≤2); contested ability<4 unchanged (`-1500`, a weak solo can't win the battle); ability≥4 unchanged; the V151/V181 contested-commit logic is gated `oppPower>0 && abilityPass`, never reachable by a sub-4 droid anyway. Invasion-flip read-only pass confirmed V156/V151/V181 reachable only by ability≥4 bodies → contested-fight discipline not regressed.
- Verified: compiles + boundary math above. STILL PENDING: live droid-deck game (a droid lands at an uncontested site; the Throne Room seeds). Running jar is old code until rebuilt. Does NOT by itself flip Invasion — that also needs a NEIMOIDIAN at the Throne Room via a `with a <type> there` parser/steering enhancement (`Handoffs/K2_HANDOFF_2026-06-25_invasion-flip-fix.md`).
- Revert: restore the gate to `if (!abilityPass)` (drop the `oppPower > 0f &&`).

## 2026-06-28 — V67h dead-search: junk-target pass-through (stop vetoing valid location pulls from multi-clause OR interrupts)
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/strategy/DeckOracle.java` + `chosenone/strategy/DeckOracle.java` (`validatePullFromSourceCard`, just before the WILL_FAIL return ~1217 / ~1110): before returning WILL_FAIL, if ANY parsed target is JUNK (`length > 25` OR contains a digit, the SAME criterion V177 uses), return UNKNOWN instead. The V67h call sites (`DeployEvaluator:789`, `ActionTextEvaluator:4318`, both bots) apply `-9999` ONLY on WILL_FAIL, so UNKNOWN lets the existing weights handle the action.
- Why: forensics over the 2026-06-23..27 logs (workflow `wz72d8p1g`) found Rando PASSING a valid location pull. Turn 1, dark: offered `Deploy a Blockade Flagship site from Reserve Deck` (from the interrupt We Must Accelerate Our Plans). Positive rules scored it +1600 (V100 +1500, V116 +100), but V67h's game-text parser collapsed WMAOP's three OR-clauses into ONE junk target ("3 force to take one effect of any kind, one interrupt with the word 'podracer s'...") and, not finding that junk in Reserve, returned WILL_FAIL → `-9999` → net `-8399` → Pass (2.0) won. Cross-checked: Blockade Flagship: Bridge WAS in Rando's Reserve and the card's filter accepts it, so the pull would have SUCCEEDED. V177 already flagged this exact text as PARSE-JUNK pass-through, but V67h applied its own `-9999` independently; the two detectors disagreed and the meaner one won.
- Boundary (additive-domination + V177 parity): genuine dead-searches use short, digit-free proper-noun targets (My Sister Has It → [chief chirpa's hut, guest quarters], Fall Of The Legend → weather vane, Welcome Home Lord Tyranus → [petranaki arena, works]) — none is junk, so they still WILL_FAIL and correctly block the reveal-the-deck waste. Only a junk parse flips to UNKNOWN. The two dead-search detectors (V177 + V67h) now agree.
- This ADJUSTS V67h in place (no new V-tag), per the update-old-rule rule. Compiles clean (`mvn -pl gemp-swccg-server -am compile`, EXIT=0). STILL PENDING: reload-ai + a real game running We Must Accelerate Our Plans (grep `nohup.out` for `V67h PARSE-JUNK pass-through` and confirm the Blockade Flagship site pull is no longer `-9999`'d). Running jar is old code until rebuilt.
- Revert: delete the `v67hJunkT` junk-gate loop just before the WILL_FAIL return in both `DeckOracle.java` files.

## 2026-06-28 — V188: Set Your Course For Alderaan — don't deploy ability characters to Death Star sites (drains canceled there)
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/common/strategy/CharacterDeploySiteEvaluator.java` `evaluateSite` (top, right after `opponentId`): early gate returns `-900` when the deploying card has ability ≥ 1, the candidate site is a `Filters.Death_Star_site`, AND the deploying player has `Set Your Course For Alderaan` in play (front). Shared `common/` file → both bots, both deploy paths (`DeployEvaluator` from-hand + `CardSelectionEvaluator` choose-target) via the one `evaluateSite`.
- Why: Steve — "When Rando is playing 'Set Your Course For Alderaan' he should not deploy his characters with ability to the Death Star because he can't Force drain there. Wasted guys deployed there last game." The objective's FRONT text: "At Death Star sites, your Force drains and battle damage against you are canceled." An ability character at a Death Star site can't drain (its whole purpose), so it's wasted; steer it to a drainable battleground.
- Front-only detection (no flip false-positive): `PhysicalCardImpl.getBlueprint()` (`:497`) returns `_backBlueprint` once flipped, so `getTitles()` / `Filters.title("Set Your Course For Alderaan")` stops matching the instant the card flips to "The Ultimate Power In The Universe" — at which point the Death Star becomes the win condition and you WANT presence there, so the gate correctly lifts on its own.
- Boundary (additive-domination): NARROW early gate, fires ONLY for (ability ≥ 1 + Death Star site + this objective front). Ability-0 fodder untouched (may still hold a Death Star site for the flip/superlaser). Every other objective, every non-Death-Star site, and the flipped back side are untouched (§A/§B/§C/§D run normally). `-900` sits clearly below a drainable battleground's net (~+600–800 = §A win +500 plus §B), so ability drivers route to drainable sites; not an absolute block (if a Death Star site is the only legal target, Rando can still go). No clean engine "drains canceled here" query exists, so we key on the objective + site.
- NEW V-tag (V187 = other K-2's concurrent work; this is the next free tag). Compiles clean (`mvn -pl gemp-swccg-server -am compile`, EXIT=0). Source-only — NOT deployed (Steve is holding reload-ai to test a different deck first).
- Revert: delete the `v188Ability` early-gate block at the top of `evaluateSite` (tagged `V188`).

## 2026-06-29 — V120 fix: weapon-pull block no longer mis-fires on a CHARACTER pull (Vader)
- MOD `server/.../rando/evaluators/ActionTextEvaluator.java` + `chosenone/.../ActionTextEvaluator.java` (V120 weapon-title match, ~1877): the bidirectional `contains()` match (added by V125 for abbreviated/prefixed weapon titles) also matched a CHARACTER pull whose name is a substring of a weapon title. "Deploy Vader from Reserve Deck" parsed `vader`; the deck's `Darth Vader's Lightsaber` title contains `vader`, so V120 classified the Vader CHARACTER pull as a weapon pull with no holder and HARD-BLOCKED it at -9999 every turn. Vader is the Hunt Down deck's flip engine, so the objective never flipped and Rando lost. Confirmed in the 2026-06-29 replay (`V120 WEAPON-PULL BLOCK: 'Deploy Vader from Reserve Deck' (weapon 'Vader', criteria 'vader') ... -9999`).
- Fix: for the loose "weapon-title contains parsed-name" direction, require the parsed name to cover the weapon title's LAST significant word (the weapon noun, e.g. `lightsaber`), not just the owner portion. `vader` → title core noun `lightsaber`; `vader`.contains(`lightsaber`) = false → no longer a weapon pull. Exact-match and parsed-name-contains-full-title directions (the V125 cases, e.g. `Vader's Lightsaber` → `Darth Vader's Lightsaber (V)`) unchanged: `vader's lightsaber`.contains(`lightsaber`) = true. Strips `(V)`/parenthetical suffixes; ≥4-char noun threshold mirrors the V185/DeckOracle idiom.
- Boundary: V120 still fires on a real weapon pull with no holder (`Deploy Vader's Lightsaber from Reserve Deck` with no Leia/Ben/Rey on table → -9999). Only the character-name false positive is removed. Adjusts V120/V125 in place — no new V-tag (update-old-rule rule).
- Verified: compiles clean (`mvn -pl gemp-swccg-server -am compile`, EXIT=0). Source-only until reload-ai.
- Revert: restore the single `if (pTitleLower.contains(v120WeaponLower) || v120WeaponLower.contains(pTitleLower)) { ... }` in both files.

## 2026-06-30 — V61c: always keep 3 cards in the Reserve Deck for battle/weapon destiny
- GOAL (Steve): "He should probably always keep 3 cards in reserve for any battle that turn." Rando kept emptying its Reserve Deck during activation, leaving nothing to draw battle/weapon destiny from, then losing battles it should have won.
- ROOT CAUSE was a conflict between two of Steve's own rules: V168 (ActionTextEvaluator) forced Rando to ALWAYS activate Force (+5000, never pass), and activating moves cards Reserve → Force Pile. So "always activate" literally drained the reserve. V61c's amount-cap alone couldn't hold the line: the engine forces ≥1 per activation (V43), so once the reserve was at 3 it eroded 3→2→1→0 over turns (`activating 1 of 3` → `1 of 2` → `1 of 1` in the log).
- FIX, two halves:
  1. `server/.../rando/evaluators/ForceActivationEvaluator.java` `calculateActivationAmount` (~186): cap the activation amount so the Reserve Deck never drops below 3 — `amount = max(1, min(maxAvailable, reserveDeck - 3))`. Handles reserve > 3 (activate down to leave 3). Logs `V61c DESTINY BUFFER (keep 3 in reserve)`. (Earlier force-pile-gated version removed — it almost never fired because Rando draws its Force to hand.)
  2. `server/.../rando/evaluators/ActionTextEvaluator.java` — NEW this session, the piece that actually makes it hold. When `reserveDeck <= 3`, carve an exception into BOTH activation guards so Rando PASSES activation instead of draining the last 3:
     - V168 always-activate block (~166): if reserve ≤ 3, score "Activate Force" at −6000 (below Pass ~5–8) instead of +5000, so the action-choice lands on Pass.
     - V38.3 "you have not activated Force — pass?" confirm (~1342): if reserve ≤ 3, honor "Yes" (pass, +9999) instead of the usual bounce-back to "No", or the pass would be undone.
- BEHAVIOR: reserve > 3 → activate, leaving exactly 3. reserve ≤ 3 → pass activation entirely this turn (Rando lives off its Force Pile) to protect the 3-card destiny buffer. Self-regulates: destiny/weapon draws deplete the buffer, the Used Pile reshuffles back in when the reserve empties, and Rando activates down to 3 again next time it has a surplus.
- TRADEOFF (Steve accepted, "for now"): when the reserve is ≤ 3 Rando gets no new Force from activation that turn, so it's slightly more passive when its reserve is low. FUTURE refinement (planned, not built): pass activation ONLY when Rando intends to battle that turn; a deploy-and-end-turn-without-battling turn can safely activate everything. That needs reading battle/deploy intent BEFORE the activate phase.
- Boundary (additive-domination): −6000 dominates the +5000 it replaces, and Pass (~5–8) sits well above it and above the −100 BAD_ACTION_THRESHOLD, so Pass wins cleanly without tripping the "all actions bad" fallback. reserve ≥ 4 leaves V168/V38.3 fully intact (always-activate unchanged). Both edits are RANDO-side only (ActionTextEvaluator + ForceActivationEvaluator in `rando/`); chosenone not touched.
- Verified: compiles clean (`mvn -pl gemp-swccg-async -am package`, EXIT=0); both new strings present in the running `web.jar` (`pass activation, keep 3 for destiny` = 1, `confirm pass, keep 3 for destiny` = 1); restarted + operational. STILL PENDING: live game confirming the reserve holds at 3 and battles get destiny (grep the log for `V61c DESTINY BUFFER: reserve=` pass lines).
- Revert: in ActionTextEvaluator restore the unconditional V168 (+5000 on "activate force") and the unconditional V38.3 (No +9999 / Yes −9999); in ForceActivationEvaluator restore `amount = maxAvailable` (V57 full).

## 2026-06-29 — V179 fix (#4): don't rank a download of a location you already HOLD above deploying it (A Good Friend / A Cunning Warrior)
- MOD `server/.../rando/strategy/DeployPhaseScript.java` + `chosenone/.../strategy/DeployPhaseScript.java` (`resolveSteps` section D + new `namedLocationInHand` helper): when the DPS bucketer classifies a [download]/reserve-pull action, a target naming a specific LOCATION classifies into the LOCATIONS bucket ONLY if that location is not already in the bot's hand (`s == Step.LOCATIONS && namedLocationInHand(gameState, playerId, target)` → skip).
- Why: replay 2026-06-29 (Rando LIGHT, Rey/Luke Saga). A Good Friend + A Cunning Warrior each [download] one of {a location, an epic event, a weapon}. Their once-per-turn download was classified as a high-priority LOCATIONS pull (DPS LOCATIONS bucket; V67ai +1800) because the target text contains "jedi village" — outranking deploying the actual Ahch-To: Jedi Village from hand (+1900, V162). So Rando FIRED THE DOWNLOAD FIRST while Ahch-To was still in hand; nothing was legal (Be With Me needs an Ahch-To location on table; the location was in hand not reserve; the weapon had no holder), the engine correctly reshuffled, and the once-per-turn download was burned. Rando deployed Ahch-To seconds later, too late; Be With Me (the epic event) never came out all game. NOT an engine/card bug (verified: `DeployCardFromPileEffect` re-checks `Filters.deployable` at resolve; Be With Me's `canSpot(AhchTo_location)` was legitimately false) — a Rando action-ordering bug.
- Fix mechanism: dropping the LOCATIONS tag moves the download out of the LOCATIONS bucket (it still classifies by its OTHER targets — the weapon → WEAPONS), so the DPS walk deploys the real Ahch-To from hand in the LOCATIONS step FIRST; the download fires in a later step once the location is on table and can then pull Be With Me.
- Boundary: `namedLocationInHand` only matches a specific named location (≥4 chars, not a bare category word — "location"/"site"/"system"/"battleground"/"docking bay"/"mobile site") against a LOCATION card actually in the bot's hand. So generic/objective location pulls ("Deploy a location from Reserve Deck") never gate (no card is titled "location"), and a legitimate location pull whose target sits in RESERVE (not hand) is untouched. Adjusts the V179 DPS location classification in place — no new V-tag (update-old-rule rule).
- Verified: compiles clean (`mvn -pl gemp-swccg-server -am compile`, EXIT=0); live in the running jar (`namedLocationInHand` in both bots' `DeployPhaseScript.class`, HTTP 200); traced against the replay case (targets [be with me, jedi village, leia's lightsaber] → jedi village gated out → steps={WEAPONS} → Ahch-To hand-deploy wins LOCATIONS). PENDING: a live Saga game (Ahch-To deploys before the download; Be With Me pulls).
- Revert: in both files restore the loop body to `Step s = stepForPullTargetText(t); if (s != null) steps.add(s);` and delete the `namedLocationInHand` helper.

## 2026-06-25 — V156 UPDATED: smart solo-deploy hold (ability/weapon/battleground aware) — entry backfilled 2026-07-01
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/common/strategy/CharacterDeploySiteEvaluator.java` (~462, logging at ~517/~522) — the old V156 turn≤2 solo guard (-300 flat) rewritten per Steve's spec. The hold now fires ONLY when ALL of: candidate is a battleground SITE (not a system, not a non-battleground), deploying card is a CHARACTER (ships/vehicles exempt — ability-2 ships are often fine solo), turn ≤ 2, and the site is NOT objective-flip-relevant (seeding flip sites must stay legal) — with a `v156FlipNotReady` carve-back: a flip-relevant site does NOT exempt when the flip is mechanically not ready yet (Verge of Greatness: Death Star not orbiting Scarif → a turn-1 solo seed is still a free kill, hold it). Solo is allowed when ability ≥ 6, or ability ≥ 5 with a matching weapon in hand (the weapon's own `getMatchingCharacterFilter()` accepts the deployer — no hardcoded names); otherwise `V156 SOLO HOLD` -600, and `V156 SOLO OK` +250 when a buddy is available to join. Added `isObjectiveRelevantSite` param to `computeTeamViability` + call site. Shared `common/` file → both bots.

- Boundary (additive-domination): -600 holds a lone sub-threshold character below the §A +500 uncontested reward (net -100 < Pass) without hard-blocking (an objective-forced deploy can still exceed it); +250 buddy nudge cannot flip a contested -1500 site positive. Non-battleground deploys (senators to the Senate etc.), systems, turn 3+, and ability-0 fodder at flip sites are all untouched.
- Committed: rode into `d72ced949` (the V188 commit swept this file). Verified live 2026-06-28+ (`V156 SOLO HOLD` fires in the log). PENDING: nothing.
- Revert: restore the pre-2026-06-25 V156 block (flat -300 turn≤2 solo guard) — the old logic is commented out in place above the new block.

## 2026-07-01 — V177 dead-search: CATEGORY RESCUE (stop false-blocking type-phrase downloads like I'm Sorry's interior Cloud City site) — adjusts V177 in place
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/evaluators/ActionTextEvaluator.java` (~261, inside the V177 block branch) + chosenone mirror (~242) — before applying the -2000 DEAD SEARCH block, consult `DeckOracle.validatePullFromSourceCard(RESERVE_DECK, sourceGameText)` — the SAME validated path V67h and ActionAudit already trust (V82.1 category fallback + V82.2 blueprint-predicate fallback). WILL_SUCCEED → no block, log `V177 CATEGORY RESCUE`, the action re-enters normal scoring. Any other outcome → the -2000 block stands unchanged.
- Why: TDIGWATT game 2026-07-02 02:09 UTC (replay `7co2xviwqo5q3zac`), Rando lost by depletion in 4 turns. I'm Sorry (V) may [download] an interior Cloud City site; Dining Room (V) + Security Tower (V) sat in Reserve ALL GAME. The raw `hasTargetInZone` title matcher cannot match the type-phrase "interior cloud city site" against titles like "Cloud City: Dining Room" (last-word "site" is in no title; the ≥6-char word-rescue only tries "interior"), so V177 declared DEAD and blocked the download 19 times while V67h said WILL_SUCCEED in the SAME 19 evaluations — an in-log self-contradiction. The block froze Rando at 1 CC site (flip needs 3 controlled Bespin locations), froze Force income at 8 vs 12, and the two downloadable sites were eventually burned as Force loss. Same false negative blocked Piett's "[any commander]" search 27x on T4. This is the detection-path-mismatch class from Rando_AI_Rule_Audit.xlsx.
- Boundary (additive-domination): NO magnitude changes — pure gate alignment. Genuine dead searches (proper-noun targets: Force Projection, Weather Vane, Petranaki Arena) still fail the validator and still block at -2000. The rescue fires ONLY on WILL_SUCCEED — exactly the verdict V67h acts on — so the two dead-search detectors can no longer disagree. Rescued actions score naturally (V183's deck-title scan is unaffected: type-phrases are not deck titles; V97/V116 positives apply as designed).
- Accepted false positive: V82.1 maps the type word only ("site" → ANY location in Reserve, qualifiers ignored). If every interior CC site is out of Reserve but any other location remains, the rescued search fires and no-ops: one wasted once-per-turn action + reshuffle, no Force cost. Bounded per-turn waste vs. the unbounded strategic damage of a permanent false block; consistent with feedback_reserve_deck_pulls (pulls fire every turn). Do NOT "improve" this by tightening `validatePullFromSourceCard` with qualifier matching — V67h and ActionAudit share it; that would change three rules at once. NOTE: `recordFailedPull` (the 2-strikes backstop) has ZERO callers anywhere in src — dead wiring — so the no-op repeats each turn; separate fix if it ever matters.
- Mirrored to chosenone (identical insert, chosenone DeckOracle types).
- Verified: compiles clean in-container; jar byte-search PRESENT in both bots' classes; work-verifier PASS 5/5. LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-07-02 (2 headless self-play games, k2_player.py holding the LIGHT seat): `V177 CATEGORY RESCUE` fired 3x on the I'm Sorry action, zero false DEAD SEARCH blocks, and I'm Sorry deployed an interior CC site from Reserve on turn 1 in BOTH games (Carbonite Chamber / Security Tower, replays 94d9142hqg0zs0fr + x5cbzmegdjsx24zv).
- Revert: delete the `V177 CATEGORY RESCUE` if/else wrapper in both ActionTextEvaluators — the original block body is the untouched else branch.

## 2026-07-04 — V189: net-value drain gate (never pay more to initiate a drain than it's worth) + V140 repaired in place
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/evaluators/ActionTextEvaluator.java` (`evaluateForceDrain`, right after the V24.15 zero-drain check) + chosenone mirror — NEW V189: query `getInitiateForceDrainCost` (the exact query the engine charges via `PayInitiateForceDrainCostEffect` — sums every INITIATE_FORCE_DRAIN_COST modifier: Battle Order, Battle Plan, anything future, no card-name hardcoding) and hard-block (-2000, early return) any drain whose initiation cost exceeds the drain amount.
- ALSO MOD same files — V140 UPDATED in place: its hand-rolled "Battle Plan on table = universal waiver" scan was factually wrong (Battle Plan, Card8_035, waives only Battle Order's, Card8_118, modifier and imposes its OWN identical 3-Force tax; only occupying a BG site + BG system waives it). Detection replaced with the same engine cost query; old scan commented out in place. Reasoning/log strings updated to the engine-truth wording.
- Why: Steve, 2026-07-04: "He should not have paid to drain for 1 with battle plan or battle order on the board." Game `20jqtseod148of4y`: with asdf's Battle Plan on table, Rando paid 3 Force to drain 1 at Audience Chamber TWICE. Offender A: the untagged "no deployables — drain is our only pressure" +70 boost returns before any net math. Offender B: V140's false Battle-Plan waiver (+60). V189 fronts both.
- Boundary (additive-domination): cost-0 games completely unaffected (0 is never > drain; drainAmount > 0 guaranteed past V24.15). Net 0 ALLOWED (pay 3 drain 3 = 3 permanent Life Force damage for 3 recycling Force). Pay-3-drain-2 now blocked — deliberately SUPERSEDES V52's old "drains >= 2, net -1, marginal but worth it" stance (AI_VERSION_HISTORY ~line 2551); Steve's rule wins. Blocked drain scores exactly -2000 (nothing scores a drain action earlier), losing to Pass (+5) with ~2005 margin; the early return skips the +70 boost / V140 / V52 / V29.9 — the offenders. V104 (drain ≤ 1 under BO) becomes a redundant backstop fronted by V189 (kept, noted in code). Known remaining gap (pre-existing, not fixed here): the untagged forceAvailable<3 branch still applies -50 to genuinely FREE drains before V140 runs — under-draining, fail-safe, separate item.
- UPDATED 2026-07-06 (in place, Steve 2026-07-04: "We should still allow drain 2 for 3 force if there is enough force to deploy and move everything that rando wants to do that turn"): two tiers now. Net <= -2 (pay 3 drain 1 — the actual 07-04 offender) stays FLAT-BLOCKED. Net -1 (pay 3 drain 2) is BUDGET-GATED: allowed iff `forcePile - initCost >= sum(live deployable hand costs, persona-dead excluded via AiCardHelper.isDeadCard) + 2 move allowance`. Drains are CONTROL phase (before Deploy/Move), so the budget is a forecast; it is recomputed from live gameState at EVERY drain decision, which satisfies "recheck whenever new force is used" by construction (DeckOracle.refresh freshness pattern). Steve's "move the deploy cost evaluator right after activation" was NOT built literally: DeployPhasePlanner caches per turn (createPlan ~116-120), so priming it at Control would serve a stale over-budgeted plan into the V38.4/waitingForPlannedCards hold-back machinery (log proof: 07-04 game, drain paid at 17:16:50, plan created 17:16:57 with force=9 not 12); per-decision recompute is semantically identical with none of that risk. Follow-up only if a second consumer needs the planner primed early. REWORD of the old boundary line: pay-3-drain-2 is budget-gated, not flat-blocked — V52's old "net -1 marginal but worth it" stance is RESTORED only while the turn's deploy+move intent stays funded. Boundary rows: drain-1-cost-3 → net -2 → blocked (both versions); drain-2-cost-3 at 12F/no plan → allowed (+70, beats Pass); 12F/5-cost plan → 9 >= 7 → allowed (V52 +50 + multi-drain +200, ceiling ~+340 with V29.9's +40+30); 6F/5-cost plan → 3 < 7 → blocked -2000; turns 1-2 under BO an allowed net -1 with deployables scores V48 -50 + multi-drain +200 = +150 and fires (pre-V189 behavior, faithful to the budget rule, NOT a regression). Known over-allow gaps (all err toward one marginal drain): forecast omits Effects/weapons/devices deploy costs, Reserve-pull costs, and opponent-turn reacts; an exception inside the budget loop fails open (net -1 proceeds ungated at logger.debug) — same fail-open class as v1. V104-fronting note stays true (under BO an allowed net -1 is drain 2; V104 only fires on drain <= 1). Grep: `V189 NET -1 DRAIN ALLOWED` / `V189 DRAIN NET-1 BUDGET BLOCK`. Revert of the update alone: restore the single `cost > drain → -2000` block.
- Mirrored to chosenone. Verified: compiles clean; jar + live-game verification pending (grep `V189 DRAIN NET-VALUE BLOCK`; a Battle Plan game should show the pay-3-drain-1 blocked while a 3+ drain still fires).
- Revert: delete the V189 block in both ActionTextEvaluators; un-comment V140's old scan and restore its old reasoning/log strings.

## 2026-07-06 — V51/V105/V112/V117 (in place): Battle Order 4th-slot deadlock + occupation-predicate unification
- Why: Steve, 2026-07-06, the "Verge of Greatness" game (replay `unli50oa1ur8bdux`): Rando controlled Scarif (battleground SYSTEM) + Scarif battleground sites and drained there 70+ times, qualifying for his own Battle Order (dark Effect 13_54, a K&D defensive shield that taxes the OPPONENT's drains +3 while Rando occupies both theaters) the entire game — and never deployed it. Two faults: (1) `ShieldStrategy.prefers4thSlot` returned "Battle Order" all game (occupation genuinely true), and the V105/V107 4th-slot code hard-blocked every OTHER offered shield at -5000 (2760 fires) to force a Battle Order that was never in the candidate list, so the 4th slot deployed nothing (a prefer-an-unavailable-card deadlock); (2) latent — the three Battle Order occupation checks (V51 deploy path, V51 shield path, V112) hand-rolled `hasBGSite/hasBGSystem` via `getCardsAtLocation` + owner-match, which disagreed with V105's power-based scan (V51 logged hasBGSystem=false while a ship sat at Scarif system). Same detection-mismatch class as the V140 fix (2026-07-04).
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/evaluators/CardSelectionEvaluator.java` + chosenone mirror. TWO new private helpers per bot:
  - `occupiesBothTheaters(game, playerId)` = `Filters.canSpot(game, null, and(occupies(pid), battleground_site)) && ...battleground_system` — the engine's own `OccupiesCondition` semantics, exactly what Card13_054 checks, so gate and card can never disagree. Fails closed (no false deploy). Replaces the hand-rolled loops in V51 (both deploy + shield paths) and V112. Old loops commented out in place (feedback_comment_out_old_rules).
  - `preferredShieldInCandidates(context, preferredTitle)` — scans the decision's full candidate list (ARBITRARY_CARDS blueprint path + standard card-id path) for the preferred title. V105 and V117 now only pursue/hard-block for the preferred 4th-slot card when it is ACTUALLY on the menu; otherwise they HOLD the slot closed (Steve's closed-by-default 4th slot) instead of spamming a -5000 block for a card that can't be picked. Kills the 2760-fire deadlock. (Steve's call 2026-07-06: "just stop the spam" — keep the 4th slot closed when Battle Order isn't offered, do not fall to a random shield.)
- Boundary (additive-domination): NO magnitude changes — the +2000 boost / -5000 hold / -9999 gate keep their values; only the boolean predicates feeding them change. When Rando occupies both theaters AND Battle Order is offered → V51 passes (+50 / no block) and V105 boosts it +2000 → it deploys. When Rando does not occupy both → -9999 (still blocked, no self-tax, no regression). When the preferred card is absent → HOLD (slot closed), no spam. Occupy-vs-control: the card triggers on OCCUPY (presence), so the gate uses occupy, matching Card13_054. Known unmodeled edge (not fixed, over-deploy not self-tax): the helper omits Battle Order's `battlePlanOnTable` waiver branch — if a Battle Plan is already on table both taxes are waived and Rando's Battle Order is redundant; deploying it then wastes a slot but does not self-tax. Noted for a follow-up.
- Verified: compiles clean in-container (both bots). Jar byte-search + work-verifier + SELF-PLAY pending: a Scarif dark deck game where Rando controls both theaters should now show `V51 ... Requirements met`, `V105/V107 4TH SLOT: BOOST 'Battle Order'`, and Battle Order reaching the table; and NO `HARD-BLOCK ... prefer 'Battle Order'` spam when it isn't offered. NOTE: whether Battle Order is actually OFFERED (K&D shield-play is 4x/game capped) is a separate limiter self-play must confirm.
- Revert: restore the four hand-rolled occupation loops (commented out in place) and remove the `preferredShieldInCandidates` guard from V105/V117 (revert the `preferredOnMenu` / `!preferredShieldInCandidates` conditions to `preferred != null` / `v117Preferred == null`); delete both helpers.

## 2026-07-06 — V51 EARLY-DEPLOY (in place): Battle Order/Plan deploys turns 1-2 when Rando occupies both theaters
- Why: Steve, 2026-07-06 (follow-up to the same-day V51/V105/V112/V117 Verge fix): "Battle Order can be deployed during turn 1 or 2 as well if a battleground site and system is occupied by Rando" + "Just have to occupy. does not matter if opponent also occupies." Battle Order (dark shield 13_54) taxes the OPPONENT's drains +3 while Rando occupies a battleground site + system, and the tax COMPOUNDS every turn — so deploying it early is high value. GAP: with occupation true but before the turn-3 4th slot (SHIELD_PACING allows 2 shields turn 1, 3 turn 2; the V105 +2000 4th-slot boost only opens at shieldsRemaining()<=1), Battle Order scored only the base SITUATIONAL_HIGH-untriggered 80 (its conditionsMet is never set — no opponentObjective/opponentCardsSeen/auto-play trigger), losing to auto-play shields (200). So it never deployed turns 1-2 even when qualifying.
- MOD `server/.../ai/models/rando/evaluators/CardSelectionEvaluator.java` + chosenone mirror — in BOTH V51 Battle Order branches (the evaluateShieldSelection shield path AND the Reserve/hand deploy path), when `occupiesBothTheaters(game, playerId)` is true, add `+200` (base 80 → 280) so Battle Order/Plan beats the auto-play shields and deploys within the turn's pacing budget. Extends the existing V51 gate in place (no new V-tag, per feedback_update_old_rule_not_new_version). Also extended both V51 title tests to match "battle plan" (the light twin), consistent with the V112 gate.
- Boundary (additive-domination, verified): +200 is GUARDED on `shieldScore > -50f` so it can NOT resurrect a V43 redundant-shield block (-100, opponent already has an equivalent Battle Order/Plan), a pacing-cap -50, or a not-played -100 — those all still win. Not-qualifying stays -9999 (the boost lives in the occupy-both else-branch only). 4th slot unchanged: the V105 +2000 still dominates (the +200 rides on top, e.g. 40+2000+200=2240, Battle Order was already the V105 pick). Occupy-ONLY gate per Steve — no opponent-occupation clause (rare over-deploy when opponent also occupies both = tax waived both ways, a slightly wasted shield, never a self-tax). Magnitude 280 beats auto-play (200) and sits under the top dark grabbers; exact turn-1 ordering is deck-dependent but it deploys by turn 1-2 within pacing.
- KNOWN GAP (documented, not fixed): the boost lives in the shield-selection + deploy routes.
